You can use the os module in Python for your goal. Here's an example of how you can do it:

```sh
# filename: list_file_contents.sh
#!/bin/sh
for file in *
do
   if [ -f $file ]
   then
      echo "File name: $file"
      cat $file
   fi
done
```

This script will iterate through all files in the current directory and print their content. The `-f` test is used to check whether a file exists and is not a directory.

To use this script, you have to save it into a file (for example, `list_file_contents.sh`), then make the file executable with the command `chmod +x list_file_contents.sh`, then run the file with the command `./list_file_contents.sh`.
